overwrite existing files in place (#4512, #3199)\n\nwrite_file and edit_file replaced an existing file by renaming a temp file\nover it. That gave the file a new inode every time, so it lost its creation\ntime, hard links and inode identity (#4512), and on Windows the rename fails\nwith EPERM when another process holds the file open (#3199).\n\nBoth now go through overwriteInPlace in lib.ts, which truncates and writes\nthrough a single handle on the existing file. Symlink safety is kept by\nthree checks: lstat refuses anything that is not a regular file, the open\nuses O_NOFOLLOW where the platform has it (POSIX), and the opened handle\nmust have the device and inode lstat saw, which catches a swap between the\nlstat and the open on every platform, Windows included. Permission bits now\nsurvive by construction, so the chmod repair after the rename is gone.\n\nThe trade-off is crash-atomicity: a crash mid-write can leave the file\npartly written. Documented in the README and the changeset.\n\nThe pinning tests for #4512 and #3199 now assert the correct behavior and\nlose their KNOWN BUG markers; new tests stage a symlink swap and a file\nswap inside the TOCTOU window against the real filesystem.\n\nApproach follows #4516 (O_NOFOLLOW open, fstat, truncate and write) and\nthe Windows O_NOFOLLOW caveat raised on #4512.\n\nPart of #5004 (Wave
